N*w Delhi, the 7th May, 1991 

S.O, 1425.—Whereas on 20th February, 1970 a scheme 
of amalgamation of the National Bank of Lahore Ltd., 
Dilhi v,im the S‘ate Bank cf India was sancroned by the 
Central CovtiiiiTient rn exercise of the powers conferred by 
and in a^c.'rdaaci v.iiL 5ection 45 of the Banking Reaula- 
lion 5ct, 1949. 

Whereas under Sub-clause (ixl of Clause 6 of the said 
Scheme, the Slate Bank of India was required to make a 
final valuation of the assets of the Nalioual Bank of Lahore 

I.td.. Delhi, which have been provisionally valued on the 
pi escribed date on the expiry of tweb'e years from the 
prescribed date. 

Whereas the State Bank of India has represented that 
in view of the large number of assets involved and the 
recovery of most of the items yet to be realised Inspite of 
its efforts, it has not been able to make the final valuation 
within the time specified in sub-clause (lx) of Clause 6 of 
the Scheme of amalgamation. 

And whereas the Central Government in consultation 
with the Reserve ll.mk of India is satisfied that a difficulty 
h.is arisen in giving effect to the scheme of amalgamation 
which It is necessary to remove by extendmg the time within 
which the final valuation of assets is required to be made. 

Now, therefore, in e.sorcise of the powers conferred by 
clause 21 of the Scheme of amalgamation dated 20-2-1970 
of the National Bank of Lahore Ltd , Delhi with the State 
Bank of India, the Central GovemnienI hereby directs that 
the State Bank of India shall m consultation with and with 
the approval of the Reserve Bank of India value the a.sscts 
of the National Bank of Lahore Ltd., Delhi which have 
not been realised and valued, within a ^rlod of twenty two 
years from the prescribed date.

prohibited and regulated areas. This shall be in addition 
to and shall not in any way prejudice the similar declara-

have been in service from 1939.

7, At the out set I must point out that the reference relat¬ 
ing to the Award Ex. W/1 is entirely different and is not 
connected with the superannuation of the workman con¬ 
cerned. The terms of reference are reproduced above. I 
need not reproduce the facts of the case in Reference No. 
29/81. However, 1 reproduce the relevant part of the 
order made in the Award in Case No, 29/.81 which is a« 
under 

“T, therefore, answer the question referred by saying 
that the workman was entitled to be regularised 
on the post of a Head Clerk from May, 1977 on¬ 
wards and his transfer to another colliery as a 
Despatch Clerk was unjustified. He would, there¬ 
fore, be entitled to the salary and all other bene¬ 
fits of a Head Clerk till hi’i date of retirement. 
The workman is entitled lo costs of Rs. 100 from 
the management." 

Thus this reference relates to entirely different issues alto¬ 
gether and would not come in the way of issuing the order 
of retirement at the age of superannuation. The workman 
has admitted that while he was working in the private 
colliery there was no agreement to the effect that pnrtiv 
cular age 6hall be the age of stipcranrmifion. He was not 
given any fresh appointment by Ihc piescnt management who 
had taken over the mines at tile time of .nationalisation. 
Thus t}ie rules or the direction Issued by the competent 
authority fn this retard would be binding on (he workman 
concerned and as per Ex. M/I the age of superanmiation

enhanced from 58 t© 60 ywr* and the workman was

the age of 60 years. ITius there w no retrenchment of 
the workman. It was not necessary to obtain the pernns- 
slon of the Tribunal before retiring tha workman. Ihc re¬ 
tirement certainly does not amount to retrenchment. The 
Complaint is not tenable in the circtimstances. Complaint

AWARO 

The Central Government by their order No. L-13011/8/ 
90-IR(DU), dated 2.3-1-1991 have referred the following in- 
dii-strial dispute to this Tribunal for adjudlcaiton under Sec¬ 
tion KXlkd) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 ; 

“Whether the action of the Cantonment Board, Pune 
in relation to all its workmen at Pltne in cancelling 
the 1st, 3rd and 5ih Saturday holidays of u month 
w.e.f. 16-9-88 and not making payment of overtime 
to the workmen enlitled for the.se holidays is justified? 

If not. to what relief the workmen are entitled?” 

2. A notice of this reference was duly served upon both the 
parties. Both the iiailics appeared before this Tribunal. How'- 
ever, the Union did not file it? Statement of Claim challenging 
the .said action of the Camonment Board, and the Canton¬ 
ment Board also did not file its Wiitten Statement in support 
of its action. 

3. However, on 11-4-199] the General Secretary of the 
Union (Sangh) filed his anpEcation stating that the Union 
does not want to proceed fuither in the matter, as they want 
the said cpte'jtion to he d'seiis-ed in the J.C.M. Meeting und 
they may he allowed to withdraw' the present reference. The 
representative of the Cantonment Board made an endorse¬ 
ment on that application that Cantonment Board has no ob¬ 
jection foi the withdrawal of this reference, 

4. Therefore, ns the Union docs not want to proceed fui- 
Ibcr ill the matter, the present reference stands disposed off 
The parties to hear their own costs of the reference, 

P. D. .M’SH-ANKAR, Presiding Officer

STANDING ORDHA No. 422 
■'"Ihe Commiasiion has considcisd ihe question ot giaaiina 
some imanciiil relief to the stall posted in the Hill and 
Border Area eonsequent on the iiiteusi.'e developmental woik 
taken up b> the Commission in these areas. It has been 
decided that the following benehts shouid oe extended to 
the officers and staff of the comm'ssion posted in the Hill 
and Border Area of the places mentioned in para (2 ) below— 

(ij The Officers and staff uho arc posted to the Hill 
and Border Areas from outside areas will be uiven 
special corapensatoty allowance equal to 3 advance 
incrcjncnt subject to a minimum of Rs. 10 p.m. 
This specjtd compensatory aliowatice will be -paid to 
them from the date of Ineir postint in the Hill and 
Border Area.s and will be withdrawn when the officers 
and staff arc transferred to are.is outside the Hill 
and Border Areas. The teriri Trom ouside areas' 
should be taken to mean ‘from ateaa outside the 
district in the. Hill and Border Areas to which they 
are posted. 

(ii) The special compensatory ailowanca mentioned above 
would be paid on furnis.'ijng an underlaklng to 
the effect that they' would be agree to serve in 
those ureas for a mmiraum period of 2 years unless 
transferred elsewhere in the intetest of Commission’s 
work. 

(iiJj In addition to. the special compensatory allowance 
indicated above, officers and staff in the Hill and 
Border Areas wilt also be entitled to the grant of 
special allowance it may be applicable to the Central 
Government employees located at the ditferent 
places in those areas. As regards special allowances 
admissible at Tripuia orders have teen issued under 
S.O.'No. 315 dated 18-6-63, 

These orders will apply in the case of Commission’s 
employees in the Hill and Border areas of the following 
places ;— 

(i) Kashmir 

fii) Himachal Pradesh 

(iii) Punjab 

(ivj Uttarakhand division 

(v) Tripura and Manipur 

(vi) Rajasthan 
>ii) Gujarat, 

This issues with the cmcurtence of the Pinanctal adviser 
to the Commission.’

AWARD 

1 . ine Central Government, Ministry of Labour, vide its 
iiouiicauon No. C-4U012|l04i88-D-41El dated 24-.s-8y^ has 
reieiiea tne following dispute for adjuuication to tffis 
iriDimai ;— 

js.ya Op M’aiiaai AoniKari ilciepiionesj Kisinkesh. ka 
isnii Anisnu rat aiugu iviazaoor ko ainank i-2-88 ' 
se sewa mukta karna nyayocmi atii / Vadi Nam 
to karani kar Ds anutosli k'a adaitiui bai V 

2 . llic workman’s case in brief is that iic was engageu 

On as a dairy rated, casual laoouf in 1988 ana Ins services' 
were termmated illegally w.e.f. 1-2-88 in yiolapon of tbe 
piovisiOns or sccuoii Inuusinal Gispmes Am. tie anc- 

6 <is mat ne nan been paid wages ut the rate of Rs. ik.jo 
paisa per day wnen he ought to have been paid wages at 
me rate or Rs. 28.25 per day. He has iiiereiore aiiegcu 
tnai the workman prayed for ins reinstaCemeni wiin full baca 
wages. ... 

3. In defence the management plead that the wuikman 
nas engaged as a daily rated casual Dbuur from oepiember 
1980 . fiiB services and the services of some otne/ daily 
rated casual labour were terminated w.e.f. i-2-88 by means 
Of notice dated 21-12-87. the managcincnt admit that 
uunng tne period of one year before tue termination oi his 
SCI vices he nad worked for more than 240 days. According 
to tne management 15 days wages by way ot retfenctunent 
compensation were offered to the workman on 31-1-88, but 
ne refused to accept the same. Iherefore, the amouiii was 
remitted by means of money order to fum on 3-2-88 but 
tne money order was received back as unpaid. In fact he 
was engaged temporarily for temporary work with the clear 
undenstanding that when his services would not be needed 
nis services would be liable to termination. 

4. In his rejoinder the workman denies services of notice 
dated 21-12-87 upon him. He also denies that retrenchment 
compensation was offered to him on 31-1-88. He further 
denies that he ever refused to accept the money order re¬ 
mitted to him by the management by means of money 
order. According to him even tetreochment compensation 
alleged to have been offered fell short of the requiremehts 
under section 25F I.D. Act. His retrenchment compehsa-^ 
tion amounted to Rs. 847.50 paisa. Then he has denied 
the receipt of arrears of wages alleged to have been paid 
to him by the management. 

5. In support of his case, the workman has filed his own 

affidavit and tiome documents. On the other hand, in sup¬ 
port of their case, the management have filed the affida-vit 
of Shri Miirari Singh SDO(T) and some documents. 

6 . In his claim statement and rejoinder the workman Im 
not spsciflcally stated as to in which month of 1986 hs was 
engaged as daily rated casual labour. In para' 3 of his 
affidavit he has admitted that he was engaged in September 


7. Ext. W.l is the copy of certificate dt. 18-7-88 showing 
the number »f his working days. The document has been 
admitted by the management, from this certificate it ap¬ 
pears that in the month of September 1986, he had worked 
for 30 days. Thus it stands proved that he was engaged 
as daily rated casual labour w.e.f. 1-9-86. 

8 . It is the admitted caee of the parties that the services 
of the workman were retrenched w.e.f. 1-2-88. The work¬ 
man has alleged violation of section 25F in his claim state¬ 
ment and voilation of section 25N in his rejoinder. No 
argument has been advanced from the side of the workman 
by his authorised representative on the alleged violation of 
section 25N Industrial Disputes Act. For the purposes of 
examining the plea of voilation of the provisions of section 
25F I.D. Act, we will have to consider two things. Firstly 
we have to see whether the workman was given one month’s 
notice and secondly, we have to see whether he was offered 
retrenchment compensation in terms of section 25F I.D. Act. 

9. The management have filed photostat copy of notice 
dt. 21-12-87 with the written statement as well as with the 
affidavit of the management wtoess. During his cross exa¬ 
mination the workman was shown fare alleged signatures re¬ 
garding receipt of notice on the original notice but the 
workman denied his signatures. He was also confronted 
with his purported signatures on two statement of the month 
of June and July 1987-1988. The photostat copies of the 
same were got filed by the management under the orders of 
the Tribunal. I have compared the disputed tiignatures of 
the workman on the notice with his signatures on his state¬ 
ment dt. 15-1-91 as a witness .and the signatures admitted 
by him as appearing on the two statements and find that 
the disputed and the admitted signatures are identical. I do 
not agree with the submissions of Shri O. P. Mathur, the 
auth. representative for the v/orkman that disputed 
signatures differ with the admitted signatures. Hence on the 
first point I hold that the workman was duly served with 
one month’s notice as provided by sec. 25F I.D. Act. 

10. On the second point the management have set up the 
case that on 31-1-88, 15 days wages by way of retrenchment 
compensation were offered to the workman who refused to 
accept the same whereupon on 3-2-88, the amount was 
remitted by money order which was received back as 
unpaid. 

11. The management witness has corroborated it .and has 
filed photostat copy of the certificate given by the postal 
authorifies with regard to remmittance of Rs. 186.75 paisa as 
retrenchment compensation. Both ihe facts have been deni¬ 
ed by the workman. 

12. In view of oath against oath, the question will have 
to be examined in the light of circumstances. Annexure 4 
to the affidavit is the photostat copy of the money order 
coupen. In the space meant for communication it is written 
as follows ;— 

15 days wages of Shri Shishu Pal Singh Casual Labour 
dispensed from the Mustor Roll. 

Thus we find that there is no mention of the fact that the 
amount of retrenchment compensation was being remitted 
by money order on account of workman’s refusal to accept 
the same on 31-1-88. 

13. Ext. W.2 is the copy of letter dt. 4-8-88 from the 
SDO(T) Rishikesh to the workman. Although in it there 
is a mention of the fact about giving of notice, there i- 
no mention of the fact that retrenchment compensation 
offered to the workman by hand on 31-1-88 and he haa 
refused to accept the same. I therefore find no substance 
in the pjea raised by the management that retrenchment 
compensation was offered by hand to the workman on 31-1-88. 
It appears that only after the expiry of the period of notice 
it was realised by the management that retrenchment com¬ 
pensation had not been paid to the workman. 

14. Let UB see in the alternative whether the compensa¬ 
tion alleged to have been offered was in accordance with 
the provisions of section 25F I.D. Act. From the rejoinder 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 25, 1991/JYAISTHA 4, 1913 [Part II— Sec. 3(ii)] 

their 




[W II—3 (ii)] 


<FT 2 % i 9 Bii »^®5 4, 1913 _ 


2363 


it appears that the workman should have been paid one 
month’s wages as rcLrenchincnt compel'saiion. But this 
does not seem to be correct when it is seen in the light of 
the workman's working days given in the certificate copy 
Ext. W.l. The certificate shows that dL.ring the period 
1-9-86 to 31-1-88 he had worked for 502 days only i.e, for 
a period of less than one year six mouths. After seeing 
that Shri O. P. Mathur did not press this point. 

15. Lastly, I come to the point whether the workman had 
been paid wages at the rate oi Rs. 12.50 paisa per day or 
at the rate of Rs. 28.25 per day. With their written ttate- 
ment, the management have filed the copy of statement 
showing payment of arrears of wages to the workman, the 
copy of statement is Ext. M.l, Shri O. P. Mathur has waiv¬ 
ed its formal proof on behalt of the workman. From this 
statement it appears that the v/orkman was paid arrears 
amounting to Rs. 7855.10 paisa by way of difference of 
wagetj. The statement makes it absolutely clear that the 
workman had been paid wages at the rate of Rs, 26 per 

day for the period ending 31-12-86, at the rate of Rs. 27 

per day for the period ending 30-6-87 and at the rate of 

Rs. 28.25 paisa w.e.f. 1-7-87. At the time of arguments 

Shri Murarl Singh SDO(T) produced before the Tribunal 
c Dv of Telegram dL 22-2-83 from the General hfanager 
Telecom Lucknow showing the revised rates of daily wages. 
After seeing it even Shri O. P. Mathur agreed that the 
arrears had been paid in accordance. So it stands proved 
that the workman has been paid his wages at the prescribed 
rate and nothing is due on this count. 

16. Since, his services were terminated in violation of 
section 25F Industrial Disputes Act the order of termination 
of his services is held as void ab initio. 

17. From the copy of notice dated 21-12-87, it appears 
that the services of daily rated casual labour engaged after 
30-3-85, were terminated because of departmental instruc¬ 
tions, There is nothing to show on record as to what 
kind of these instructions are. Therefore, the workman is 
entitled to reinstatement. 

18. Held that the action of the management in terminat¬ 
ing the services of the workman w.e.f. 1-2-88 was neither 
legal nor Justified. The workman is consequently held e'lliH- 
ed to reinstatement with full back wages. Since he is gett¬ 
ing the relief on account of non compliance of full provi¬ 
sions of section 25F I.D. Act, the period w.e.f. 1-2-88 till 
the date of his reinstatement will not bo coimted towards 
Seniority, It will be Open to the rnanagement to dispensed 
with his serviCCi after making full compliance of the pro 
visions of section 25 Industrial Deputes Act, 1947. 

19. Reference is answered accordingly.

AWARD 

The Central Government in the Ministry of Labour vide 
its Order No. L-42012jl21188-D.II(B) dated 24-5-89 has 
refeared thei following industrial dispute to this Tribunal for 
adjudication : 

‘(Whether the action of the Management of Central 
Public Works Department (Elect.) in not declaring 
Shri Satya Narayan Sharm^ Temp: Khalasi as 
Quasi-Pcnnanent/Permanent is just and fair ? If 
not, to what relief the workman concerned is 
entitled 7” 

2. The workman in his statement of claim alleged that 
he was appointed ns Khalasi in imskiUed category w.e.f. 
28-3-72. The Management of the CPWD made applicable 
CCS (TS) Rules 65 to the temporarily employed work charged 
staff who opted for pensionary benefits w.e.f. 18-12-75 or 
the date on which he completed three years of service which¬ 
ever was later. Tlio workman submitted option for pen* 
slonary benefits to the Executive Engineer (Elect.) Faridabad 
vide application dated 29-10-72 which was July received hy 
the concerned authorities and receipt thereof was returned 
duly signed dated 7th February, 1973. persons junior in 
the category of Khalasi unskilled wgfe declared quasi-perma¬ 
nent by the said management and the workman concerned 
even being senior was discriminated against. Persons were 
declared even permanent who were junior to the workman 
w.e.f. 1-4-77 whefcas the workman was discriminated against 
in the matter of permanency also. The \\orkman had been 
continuously and invariably' reminding the Management to 
declare him quasi-permaneint/permanent but of no avail. 
The workman was an active member of CPWD Mazdoor 
Union and the Management indulged in unfair labour prac¬ 
tice by way of teasing, harassing and victimising the work¬ 
man ny not declaring him quasi-permanent w.e.f. 18-12-75 
and permanent from 1-4-77. He has been put to extreme 
financial hardship on account of non-grant of this status and 
has been deprived of consequential benefits of earned leave, 
half pay lea^'e seniority etc. etc. 

3. The Management in its reply admitted his appointment 
W.e.f. 28-3-72. He was not found fit by the DPC and 
hence could not be declared as quasi-peimanent. Since he 
was not found fit even for quasi-permanent the question of 
declaring him permanent did not arise. Ho was Informed 
about this fact from his Assistant Engineer vide letters dated 
8-1-82 and 4-2-82. Hence no discrimination has been done 
against the workman in this case. 

4. The Management in support of its case produced letter 

dated 17-3-82 and dated 25-10-89 Ex. M-1 and M-2 in 
support of its case while the workman representative pro¬ 
duced doertmenfs Ex. W-1 and W-2, W-3, W-4 and 

W-5. No oral evidence was, however, produced by any of 
the parties. 

5. I have heard the representative for the parties and 
liave gone through the record. 

6 . The. representative for the manapenrent has urged that 
the Departmental Promotion Committee comprising of 
officers of the Department have to declare the person fit to 
he made cniasi-prrmapent. On the acriiitinv to re: -'Tiirrendation 
made bv the concerned official is put in (hot 
category. The workman is question was never iccommeTi- 
ded for that be the DEC and if he vvns not declared fit. 
Tdie question of his absorption in the quasi-permanent or 
permanent cadre does not arise. He was dfchrcd quasi 
permanent w.e.f. 1-1-88 os pet W-5 an,' from If.nt dare on¬ 
es ard he is he-rT^- paid accordingly. 

7 The renrcscntatlve for the vvorkman on (he other hand 
has urged that the workinan had given his option while 
Ex, W-1 dated 79-10-77 for the pensionary benefits Per¬ 


sons junior to him were made quasl-pertnanent and perma- 
nent but for reasons best known to the management lie was 
not givCta that status. The representative has further refer¬ 
red to the circular of the GOI CPWD bearirtg No. 32f 17/77- 
EC’X d*u_-d 18-8-87 according to which quasl-permanent 
status may be granted to the eligible temporary work charge 
employees w.e.f. 18-12-75, or w.e.f. the date of completion 
of 3 years of continuous service which ever was later. The 
workman In question had completed 3 years and, therefore, 
he should have been given that status immediately on com¬ 
pletion of the said period. 

8 . After having gone through the record of this case 
produced before me I am of the view that the workman 
has not come into the witness box to depose about the 
facts of this ease. The circular No. 32|l(77-ECX dated 
lS-8-81 was also to be made applicable to eligrblc temporary 
work charge employees. Whether the workman was eligible 
or not has not been established on the record of this case. 
The workman has not come to the witness box to state that 
he was ever considered by the DPC and was found fit. 
Later on, when he has been found fit by the DPC be has 
been declared quasi-permanent from Tl-88, Eligibility and 
suitability ha.s to be properly evaluated by the DPC and a 
person does nui become automatically quasi-permanent 
and permanent. I he persons junior to him had been declared 
quasi-pormanent because they were found fit. In the absence 
of any evidence on record or even the statement of the 
workman I do not find any ground to hold that the action 
of the Management of the (2PWD was in any way unjusn 
and unfair. Parties are left to bear their own costs.

2. The case of the workman is that be bad worked as 
Water Pump Operator in thei Cantonment Board Meerut 
Cantt w.e.f, l-S-M to 31-7*85, whereafter his services were 
Hlegaly terminated in voilation of the provisions of section 
25-F and Section 25-G of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. 
By that time be bad worked for more than 240 days. At 
the time of termination of his services, S/Shrl Nurendra 
Kumar, Chandan and Rajiv who were junior to him were; 
retained In service. Not only that after the termination of 
his services S/Shri Rajiv and Ajai were employed by the 
Cantonment Board as Pump Attendants. He has, therefore, 
prayed for his reinstatement with continuity of service, back 
wages and with aU other consequential benefits. 

3. With regard to the period of employment and the 
capacity in which the worionan had work^, the manage¬ 
ment has partly admitted the facts without stating specifi¬ 
cally as to what facts have been admitted in para two of the 
written statement. The management plead that the Cantone- 
ment Board has to eugage temporary hands from time to 
time depending on the project. The workman did work on 
temporary basis. The completion of project necessitate the 
era^oycr to ask temporary hands not to report for dntyjny 
more. All this does not amount to retrenchment or termi¬ 
nate of service. With regard to retention of S|Shri Sanjiv 
Kumar Chandan and Rajtv and employment of S/Shrl Sanjiv 
and Ajai after the termmatlon of the workmen, the manage¬ 
ment plead that in July 1985, the Cantonement Board re¬ 
quested the Employment Exchange to recommend the names 
of persons to he appointed for a period of one year. The 
names of above named persons were sponsored by the Em¬ 
ployment Exchange. The copy of letter sent in this regard 
to employment exchange is aunexuro I to the written 
statement. 

4. The workman has filed his rejoinder. He alleges that 
the management is guilty of unfair labour practice as the 
management used to employ temporary w .ftr.'an with a 
view to deprive them of their legitimate benefits. Accord¬ 
ing to him all along he had worwd as Pump Attendant. 
Ho denies that he had been engaged specifically for any 
particular pj'oject. He also denies that he was engaged for any 
specific period Of time. 

5. In support of his case, the workman has filed his affl- 
davW and In support of inelr case the management have 
filed the affidavit of Shri Navln Chand Pant Account* Qerk 
and one document. Workman has also filed a few docu¬ 
ments. 

6. Firstly we have to see whether the workman had worked 
Ds Water Pump Operator or ho had worked m some other 
capacity. 

7. In para 2 of his claim statement he has alleged that 
he had worked as Wafer Pump Operator. He has supported 
It by means of his affidavit. But he has belied his own case 
by stating in para 1 of his rejoinder that all along he had 
worked as Water Pump Attendant. In his cross examination 
he has deposed that be does not possess any Technical quali¬ 
fication. He is simply Intermediate pass. 

8. Since in their written statement the management have 
not stated as to in wbat capacity the workman had actually 
worked, I am inclined to hold In view of the facts stated 
by the workman in para 1 of his rejoinder that ho had 
simply worl cd as Water Pump Attendant and not ns Water 
Pump Operator. I may state here that in hi» ci -ss exami¬ 
nation. the management witness has deposed that he worked 
as Assistant to Pump Operator and his job whs simply to 
dig earth and to proviefe ptees by lifting the same to the 
Pump Operator. Imnce on this point I hold that the work¬ 
man was simply a Water Pump Attendant 

9. Secondly we have to see for how many days ho had 
worked. The case set up by him In his claim statement 
and corroborated by him by means of his affidavit is that 
bo had worked from 1-8-84 to 31-7-85, There is no specific 
denial of this fact by the management In their written 
statement. For the first time from the side of tho manage¬ 
ment it has been stated by the management witness that the 
workman had never worked regularly during the period 
August 1984 to July 1985. In his cross examination, tho 
management witness has deposed that during the period In 
question ho had not worked continuously. In support of this 


fact no records have been produced by the management. In 
the absence of primary evidence and in view of any speciflo 
deniiil made by ihe management in this regard in their written 
statement I believe the workman and hold that he had 
Worked from 1-8-84 to 31-7-85, 

10. It thus becomes clear that during the period of one 
preceding year from the date of tenninatlon of his services 
he had worked for more than 240 days. On 18-3-91. the 
workman filed two documents which are the copies of certi¬ 
ficates regarding the number of working days. These me 
Ext. W-1 and W-2. These two certificates shovv that he 
had worked for 301 days during the period August 1984 
to July 1985. Naturally therefore in his case the provisions 
of Section 25-F were attached. Tlie management witness 
has admitted in para 4 of his statement in his cross exami¬ 
nation that no notice of termination of service was given 
to the workman nor he was paid any retrenchment compen¬ 
sation. Thus there was no compliance of the provisions 
of Section 25-F Industrial Disputes Act. It being so the order 
uf termination is held as illegal. 

11. The workman has also alleged violation of Section 
25-G Industrial Dilutes Act, 1947. His case is that S/Shri 
Narendra Kumar Chandan and Rajiv who were far junior 
to him were retained in service while his services wore 
terminated. In his cross examination the workman has 
deposed that when he was kept on daily waaes by the 
Cantonment Board, his name was sent to the Cantonment 
Board by tho Employment Exchange, He admits that 
tho names of the above named three persons were 
however sponsored by the employment exchange. Ext. 
M-1 Is the copy of letter written by the Executive Officer, 
Cantonement Board Meerut Cantt to the Employment Officer 
Meerut for sponsoring the names of the candidates for filling 
up temporary posts Of Motor Pump Attendant. It was dearly 
stated that the posts were only upto 31-3-86. For Motor 
Pump Attendants the requisho qualifications has been des¬ 
cribed as Vin pass with Trade Certificate in Electrician 
conversant with the duties of Motor Pump Attendant There 
is no evidence that these) three persons are still continuing 
In service. From these facts it comes out that their appoint¬ 
ment was made by following due procedure of reoinitment 
while no such procedure was followed in the case of work¬ 
man. So it cannot bo held that there was any voilation of 
the provisions of Section 25-0 on the part of the manage¬ 
ment. 

12. It Is also the case of the workman that after the 
termination of his services S/iairl Sanjiv and Ajai weio 
employed as Pump Attendant. On this point he does not 
seem to have any good case. In his cross examination he 
has admitted that tho names of these two persons were 
sponsored by the Employment Exchange and further they 
had worked in the Cantt. Board even prior to 1-8-84. There¬ 
fore it is not a case of violation of the above said provisions, 
fa relying on the provisions of Section 25-H InJustrial Dis¬ 
putes Act, 1947 read with Rules 78 of the 1. D. Rules 
(Central) 1957. Being efnploycea older than the workman 
they had a preferential right under the said provision. There¬ 
fore it is not a case of violation of the above said provisions. 

13. The ordinary relief In case of violation of the provi¬ 
sions of Section 25-F, I. D. Act is of reinstatement with 
full back wages. However, In the instant case h has been 
argued by Shri B, C. Tondon, the auth. representative tor 
the management that from the evldeince on record it will 
appear that the workman was employed simply for n parti¬ 
cular project and so after the completion of the project he 
can h.ive no claim of reinstatement. In this connection 
he has referred to facts deposed to by tho management wit¬ 
ness In his cros.s examination. The management witness 
has admitted that Cantonment Board has Its own 
permanent labour but when the work load increases casual 
iabonr is also engaged. In similar circumstances for the 
project of laying down pipes in ward No, 7 the workman 
was engaged. His job was simply to heir) the Pump Operator 
who lays the pipes. The job consisted of digging earth 
.•'nd handing over pipes to the Pump Oper.ator. According 
In the witness the work in ward No, 7 had finished. 

14. Shri Tondon has also submitted that at the most the 
workman should be awarded some compensation. From para 
6 of the statement in cross examination of the workman it 
is clear that for the last about 2 years be has been working 
in Photosfudio, Meerut Cantt. on a salary of Rs. 500 per 
month. He has admitted that while working on dally wage 
in the Cantt. Board he was simply getting Rs. 390 as wages 




[wr II—3(ii)]

'flTOT'FTTrsrw25, i 991 /^e 4, 1 yia 


per montli. Shrl Tondon has also drawn my al'ention to the 
fact that the workman Is also guilty of delay. The r^e- 
rence order it of 17th August, 1989. It meads that ho bad 
raised the dispute very late. 

15. On the other hand, ft has been alleged by Shrf N. K. 
Verma the authorised representative for the workman that 
the work in Cantonment Board never ends. If one project 
finishes the other project starts. 

16. After hearing the two sides and conS'Jering the 
facts and circumstances I am of the view that it is a fit 
case where instead of reinstatement compensation should 
be awarded to the workman. Presently ho is gainfully em¬ 
ployed. Therefore he it awarded Rs. 10,000. as compensa¬ 
tion in lieu of reinstatement. 

17. Reference is answered accordingly.

2. This is a case one Shri D. M, Singh Ex-Office Superin¬ 
tendent, Grade II, C-O.D. Jabalpur. Ho was retired with 
effect from 12-10-1983 on the basis of the chargesheet to 
the effect that he has made a false L.T,C, claim. The charge-

In that the said Shri D. M. Singh while functioning as

back by First Class rail accommodatioj] during the period

tion tickets, be gave a. written statement dated 11 February 
82 that be got the reservation through the travelling conductor 
of flr.st class both during onward and return journey and

Shri D. M. Singh by his above act exhibited conduct 
unbecoming of a Government senrant in violation of rule 3 
of CCS (Conduct) Rules, 1964. 

3. The departmental enquiiy was held against die work¬ 
man and he v^as compulsory retired with effect from 13th

4. According to the workman, he has been kept under

6 . It was hastly closed on 18-5-1983. Even thereafter

quential benefits.

be required to forthwith report to the appointing authority 
the circumstances in which the order has been made and 
obtain his ex-post facto approval in this regard. It Is no¬ 
body’s case that ex-post facto approval was not obtained. 
Thus it cannot be said that the suspension was illegal and 
ehqtiiry is vitiated oh this point. 

9. Other points are not material though D.E. file does

proceedings.

exerted any influence or coercion on hiffi or obtaining Exts. 
M2 and IdS. It is to'be noted that the delinquent has ejipilain- 
ed that the crime was committed hy him becaqso. of the ^an- 
cial crisis he was then confronting and Exts. M2 and M3 
were written hy the delinquent in his own hand wrifmg. In 
these circumstances I ‘find that the contention of the delin¬ 
quent that he hbS written-Exs.; M2 and M3 on account of 
coercion and imdue influence exerted by the Branch Manager 
on him will not hold good. On perusal of the testimony of 
MWl, the Manager of the Branch (in the enquiry) under 
whom, the delinquent was working as a peon and the depo¬ 
sition of MW2 (in the enquiry), the Senior Superintendent, 
Thrissur Zonal Office, would amply prove that the delinquent 
has committed the misconduct of theft and their versions 
are corrqborated with the testimony of MWs. 3 to 6 (in 
the enquiry) on all material particulars of the miscon¬ 
duct, As held hy the enquiry officer, theie is no reason to 
disbeUevis the version of these witnesses. Exts. M4, M5 and 
M6 in the enquiry are specific complaint letters addressed to 
the Branch Wfanager, by the complainants Sri George T. 
Parambeth, Shri N. L. Francis and Smt. K. N. Valsala 
Kumarl respectively. Ext. M7 is also a complaint letter 
written “by seven staff members of the Bank to the Manager 
regarding the theft of Shri A. N. K. Devassy, and Exts. M2 
and M3 are the letters written by the delinquent in his own 
handwriting the misconduct In these circumstances, in the 
light of the depositions of witnesses MWs. 1 to 6 and Exts. 
M2 and M3 it can safely come to. the concluritm that the 
delinquent has committed the misconduct as alleged. Theie- 
fore the finding of the Enquiry Officer that the delinquent 
was guilty of the charge is fully support^ by legal Bnd pro¬ 
per evidence and so the finding of the enquiry officer is also 
correct, 

10 ..In the result It is hereby found ffiat there was a pro¬ 
per and valid domestic enquiry and the finding of the enquiry 
officer is also correct." 

III. The question remaihs.to’ be adjudicatad Is regarding 
the justifiability and , the propriety of the punishment of dis¬ 
missal imposed on the delinquent for the misconduct Of pU- 
ferring amounts from the drawer and bags of other staff 
members of the bank kept In the dining room. In the enquiry 
it is proved that the delinquent was pUferring amounts belong¬ 
ing to other co-workers and that too within the premises of 
.the Bank and this act of the workman is h. grave misconduct. 
In view of the fact that the delinquent has committed the 
misconduct of theft of money belonging to other co-workers, 
he is not deserved any lenient punishment and the punishment 
imposed on him cannot be -jaid to be disproportionate to the 
gravity of the misconduct cothmitted by him. The order 
dismissal is only commesurable with the misconduct commit- 
led by him. Hence I find that there Is no reason for invoking 
Sec._ II-A of the I.D. Act for interfering in the matter or 
punishment Imposed on the delinquent in this case. 

IV. In the result an award is passed conforming the dis¬ 
missal of Shri A.N.K. Devassy from service w.e.f. 9-6-1987 
Shri Devassy is not entitled to any relief in this reference. 

R. RA'VEENDRAN, 'Presiffing Officer 

[No. L-120n/26/88-DI(B)] 
S. C. SHARMA, Desk Officer 

Ernakulam, 2-4-1991

New Delhi, the 6th May, 1991 

SO. 1478.—^Whereas Messrs National Dairj' Development 
Board, Anand (Gujarat) 388001 and its branches at Bombay, 
Delhi, Calcutta & Bangalore (hereinafter referred to as the 
said establishment) has applied for exemption under clause (a) 
of sub^section (1) of section 17 of the Employees’ Provident 
Funds and Miscellaneous Provisions Act, 1952 (19 of 1952) 
hereinafter referred to as the said Act). 

And whereas in the opinion of the Central Government ^e 
rules of the Provident Fund of the said establishment with 
respect to the rates of contribution are not less favourable 
to employees therein than those specifled in Section 6 ot the 
said Act and the enmloyees are also in enjoyment of other 
provident fund benefits which on the whole are not less 
favourable to thei employees than the benefits provided under 
the said Act or under the Employees’ Provident Funds 
Scheme, 1952 (hereinafter referr^ to as the said scheme) in 
relation to the employees in any other establishment of a 
similar character; 

Now, therefore. In exercise of the powers conferred by 
clause (a) of sub-sSetion (1) of section 17 of the said Act 
and subject to the conditions specified In the Schedule annex¬ 
ed hereto the Central Government hereby exempts the said 
establishment from the operation of all the provisions of the 
said Scheme. 

THE SCHEDUJ.E 

1. The employer In relation to the eaht establishment fhall 
provide for such facilities for inspection pud pay such Ins¬ 
pection charges as the Central Government may from time to 
time direct under clause (a» cf sub-section (3) of section 17 
of said Act within 15 days from the close of every month, 

2. The rate of contribution payable under the provident 
fund rules of the establishment shall at no time be lower than 
those payable under the said Act in respect of the unoxempted 
establishments and the said Scheme framed thereunder; 

3. In the matter of advances, the scheme of the exempted 
establishment shall not be loss favourable than Employees 
Provident Fund Scheme, 1952. 

4. Any amendment to the said Scheme this is more bene¬ 
ficial to the employees than the existing rules of the estab¬ 
lishment shall be made applicable to them automatically no 
amendment of the rules of the provident fund of the s^d 
establishment shall be made without the previous approval of 
the Regional Provident Fund Commissioner and where any 
amendment is likely to affect adversely the Interest of the 
employees of the said establishment, the Regional Provident 
Fund Commissioner shaU, before giving his approval, give a 
reasonable opportunity to the employees to explain their point 
of view, 

5. All employees (as defined In section 2(f) of the said 
Act) who would have been eligible to become members of 
the Provident Fund had the establishment not been granted 
exemption shall be enrolled as members, 

6. Where an employee who Is already a member of the 
Employees’ Provident Fund (Statutory) or a Provident Jhmd

24. Notwithstanding anything contained in the provident 
fund rules of the establishment, if on the cessation of any 
individual, from the membership of the fund consequent on

rules of the establishment are less favourable as compared

borne* by the employer. 

25. The employer shall bear all the expenses of the ad¬ 
ministration of the Provident Fund including the mainten¬

26. Thei employer shall display on the notice board of 
the establishment, a copy of' Ihe rules of the fund as ap¬ 
proved by the appropriate authority and as and when amend¬ 
ed thereto alongwith a translation of the salient points 
thereof in the language of the majority of the employees. 

27. The “appropriate Government” may lay down any 
further conditions for contiriued exemption of the establish¬ 
ment. 

28. The employee shall enhance the rate of provident 
fund contributions appropriately if the late of provident 
fund contribution for the clu‘s o'', establishments m which 
his establishment fells is enhanced under the said Act so 
that the benefits under the Provident Fund Scheme of , the 
establishment shall not become loss favourable than the 
benefits provided under the said Act. 

29. The exemption is .liable to be cancelled for violation 
of any of the above conditions.
